 Submerged (Isabel Soto remix)
 Review: Blue Hour's new remixes EP takes four cuts from the label boss's debut album and sends them into darker, more club-focused terrain. AgainstMe strips 'Hidden Passage' back to its skeletal rhythms, while Downside draws 'Lost Beneath the Leaves' into a hypnotic haze of rolling bass and shifting textures. Connor Wall brings more insistence and forward drive on 'Portal', before Isabel Soto closes with the spacious, reflective tones of 'Submerged'. Each remix takes a different route through the original material that gives Selva a fresh lease of life. … Read more

 Thomas Schumacher - "When I Rock" (ADHS remix) (6:08)
 Review: 90s Belgian project Cherrymoon Trax has had a large rotating cast of members with just Bjorn Wendelen, Thibault Hendrix and Nicolas Geniets remaining. Now their 'The House Of House' gets remixed by German producer Thomas Schumacher, whose 'When I Rock' gets a flip on the reverse. Schumacher updates his 1994 remix of the Belgian club classic with a fresh club-focused edge and A.D.H.S. takes on their remix with contemporary production added to a track that helped bridge hard techno and breakbeat-influenced sounds in the late 90s. A concise two-tracker that connects different chapters of Schumacher's catalogue. … Read more

 (D)lirium
 Review: Info on the Russian producer Curity is shrouded in mystery but this EP on Acquit is enough to suggest whoever they are, they know how to craft seriously sophisticated techno. 'Synthetic Mind' is deep and cavernous with deft pads and beautiful chords swirling over the dusty drum work. 'Standart Routine' picks up the pace with bumpier drums but still warm atmospheres and 'Ephemeral' is another inviting, immersive deep techno sound with gorgeous synth work. '(D)lirium' shuts down with a classy touch of Motor City steel. … Read more

 Sidewalk Tales
 [link: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1177060-01-02-03.mp3] [audio: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1177060-01-02-03.mp3] 
 Octacid (Cosmiic version)
 Review: Barcelona-based Italian producer Andrea Dama is due to release his sophomore long-player 'Zero Networks' next month, but to tide you over in the meantime there's this five-track EP for Spanish label Hypersoft. The EP opens with the trippy techno of 'Music Is So Special', before 'Logica Eversiva' swoops in to ramp up both the BPM and the tension. After that, there's more no-nonsense club techno to come in the form of the acid-tinged
 'The Absence (Live 909 Mix)', before the tempo drops considerably for 'Sidewalk', which has something of a dystopian, Cold War kinda feel. Completing the EP is 'Octacid (Cosmiic Version)', a one-way trip into the weirded-out zone with its muffled beats, acid scribbles and warping FX.

 A Machine Will Never Do X (4:55)
 [link: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF760910-01-02-02.mp3] [audio: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF760910-01-02-02.mp3] 
 F#UK The World To The Core (7:32)
 Review: Swiss producer Dan Piu is a true techno veteran, with an expansive catalogue that reaches back to the early '90s. His return to form in recent years has been a delight, with classic tracks and new productions alike resurfacing for a new generation of heads to fawn over. Following recent drops on Childhood Intelligence, Ovnie and Common Dreams, Piu rocks up to Hizou Deep Rooted Music with some stellar machine musings. "Quantum Computing" is a warm and soulful house groover with rich layers of synth work at its core, while "Robots In Rage" takes a more angular approach with skittering techno rhythms and plenty of analogue synth wriggles. "A Machine Will Never Do X" is pristine electro, and "F#UK The World To The Core" completes the set with a sumptuous ambient techno trip oozing sophistication and vintage atmospheres in equal measure. … Read more

 Nectar - "Fairy Trails" (7:21)
 [link: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1164702-01-02-02.mp3] [audio: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1164702-01-02-02.mp3] 
 Nectar - "What Tha?" (5:57)
 Review: Tokyo's Realrockdesign collective marks a full 30 years of cross-disciplinary creativity with the first of a planned trilogy. The A-side hands over a crown jewel from West Coast legend DJ Garth's Grayhound catalogue in 'Twenty Minutes Of Disco Glory', which gets remixed by DJ Harvey with a slow, deep, dark and come-to-me groove with a fizzy motif lingering like a trapped bee. The flip turns to LA veteran and Private Parts family member Nectar, who shows why he's in such demand here: his 2006 cut 'Fairy Trails' is a deliciously psychedelic descent into the underworld that perfectly befits its title. 'What Tha?' is paranoid and unsure with a writhing bassline, detuned synths bringing a freaky edge and a dubby, shuffling groove that is as inviting as the rest of the track is wonderfully weird. … Read more

 JakoJako - "Nodus" (DJ Nobu remix)
 [link: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1184421-01-01-04.mp3] [audio: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1184421-01-01-04.mp3] 
 DJ Nobu - "Wax & Wane" (JakoJako remix)
 Review: Japanese techno stylists DJ Nobu and JakoJako bring their meticulous approaches to sound design and transcendental rhythm together for a four-track exchange on the former's Bitta label. Each contributes an original before reworking the other's material, so this is a tight meeting of exploratory minds and dancefloor purpose. Nobu's 'Wax and Wane' pairs melancholic synth figures with weighty, natural-sounding drums, while JakoJako's 'Nodus' takes a more futuristic route through modular textures and rugged rhythms. Their respective remixes offer alternative perspectives, with Nobu drawing out the melodic heart of 'Nodus' and JakoJako reshaping 'Wax and Wane' into a hypnotic mesh of percussion and synths. Top notch.

 The Spirit (6:35)
 Review: Over the years, countless bands and producers have decided to call themselves Dust. The latest to don the alias are a previously unheard duo from the Netherlands who appear to have self-released this pleasingly assured debut EP. They set the tone with 'OG', where dystopian, effects-laden spoken word snippets, bubbly melodies and mind-mangling electronics ride a moody electro groove, before dropping the acid-flecked tech-house-funk of 'Oh Yeah'. 'Downtown Owl' is a Den Haag-ready slab of dark-room 4/4 electro moodiness, while 'The Spirit' is a woozy, futuristic and mind-bending slab of acid-fired electro wonkiness. Mark our words: we expect more goodness to come from these talented newcomers. … Read more

 Vapordub
 Review: Brotherly duo G Prod have spent more than a year refining the tunes on this outing for Acquit. It comes after the French artists have established themselves on labels like R&S Records and is another serene, sophisticated blend of deep electronics, deep dub and deep house. To sum up - it's deep, but also subtly melodic: 'Balsamic' is sub-aquatic silkiness, 'Alnitak' is a propulsive trip through the stars, 'Ionos' has twinkling aurora borealis-style keys over a taut techno rhythm and 'Nano B' has electro kinetics and warped acid as the pressure ramps up toward the end of the EP. Very tasteful and highly atmospheric. … Read more

 EV-6
 Review: Aleksandr Martinkevic, better known as grad_u, has been DJing and producing in Vilnius for over 20 years, and his Scale Limited empire, home to the Greyscale and Redscale labels, has run since 2012 with photographer Rima Prusakova. This 2018 entry in the red series is two long dub-techno cuts: "EV -2" and "EV -6" are full of wonderfully offset chords, stretched delays and a sub-bass you feel in your sternum. Tiny filter shifts keep the stillness restless and make this meditative, dark and club-ready. It's the sound of a man who sweats the detail. … Read more

 Namiura (5:07)
 Review: Osaka-based producer Toru Ikemoto makes his debut on NDYUKA RHYTHMS and chooses to draw on Japanese heritage and marry it with the label's percussion-led sound. Each composition carries a Japanese title with its own concept translated into carefully considered musical form. Rather than simply placing cultural references over established techno rhythms, Ikemoto develops a cohesive sound in which his background and the label's rhythmic identity form something new. From the hurried funk of 'Hayashi' to the deft loops and implicit tension of 'Namiura,' this is an elegant, purposeful collection from Osaka. … Read more

 Sub Level (5:43)
 Review: The Berlin producer Alexander Kowalski is back with four tightly sequenced jams for Frameworks, and they strike a balance between driving techno and darker, more inward moments. The title track stomps out of the block as a warm, howling wind and blizzard of fizzing hi-hats anchored by big, sweaty drum kicks. 'No Exits (Process)' subtly reshapes its contours and ramps up the textures while 'Motion' brings a shift into something leaner and meaner but still drenched in the same oversized percussion. 'Sub Level' takes things deeper and darker and to the heart of an underworld in turmoil. Great tools, made well. … Read more

 22 (5:56)
 Review: Uncoiled returns with a serious slab from label head Lonefront. His techno is deep, marbled with colour and backlit with a neon glow that lends in an otherworldly, distinctly futuristic feel. '52' is a supple shuffle between synths, bass and rubbery drums, while '25' creaks and groans more under the weight of more automated machinery. '74' cuts loose, tumbling and stumbling around the grid with clangs and detuned daubs all making it feel as though the tune might collapse at any moment. '22' shuts down with a hurried, overlapping drum line and beams of light that reflect off the percussion. It's impossibly tight and loopy with no let up, meaning your mind eventually gives up and melts down. … Read more

 I2
 Review: Techno pioneer Jeff Mills incurs spine chills with his latest collection of 'Gamma Rays' - four previously unreleased tracks recorded around 1995 and specifically made for a DJ mix film and audio recording made at Tokyo's Liquid Room. Finalised only onto 1/4" reel-to-reel tape at the time, these went unheard for three decades until their exhuming for this release. These are techno armaggedons beyond the usual eagerness-to-dominate; Mills softens the kicks to abstraction, achieving pure, flow-state zones. 'I4' and 'I2' sound rather a long way from human, the latter's interruptive stutters especially recalling those heard in his legendary live set. … Read more

 Review: Hi-hats become flange-swirled tuned elements; snares spit long sprinklets of liquid tail and melt into the phase haze. These ambient electro cuts are like cocaine. In 1994 UK emotive techno pusher Nuron/Fugue (Nurmad Jusat) singled himself out as a like mind (not surprisingly - this isn't a duo project despite appearances) and ran with the concept for the eventual six-track series' name, Likemind. Now the way-back split EP now gets a reissue, and presumably hearkens others to be reissued to come, through Blueprint Archives. The Fugue tunes are certainly a lot more relaxing, spelling out quite clearly the differences in intent and outcome between the two monikers. … Read more

 Time Will Tell (4:02)
 Review: Frequency is an alias of the Dutch techno legend Orlando Voorn, and for this one he links with Speed - a name we aren't familiar with. To the music, then: 'Hand Of The Church' has a blend of 90s house and US garage in the neon baselines that glow like distant headlights as they roam through warm, deep drums. 'Control The Phantasy' is quicker and tighter - a smooth cruiser that gives furtive glances; then 'Contrast' is a Millsian slice of space techno. 'Time Will Tell' stays in the cosmos but rides a born bat that loops back on itself as twinkling synths and a sci-fi aura all circle around as you bust your best moves. Four different sounds here, all executed with style. … Read more

 Heike (Mood mix)
 Review: Chilean minimal monster Ricardo Villalobos has got many anthems in his arsenal, but he likely never expected to. His sound, at its best, is delightfully abstract, unhinged and otherworldly, but there's something about the way certain tunes burrow deep into the brain that makes them pure club crack. 'Heike' from 1998 is one of them: the main hook is a detuned synth line that stumbles through the mix like a drunk uncle, and it's the most ravey moment in his catalogue. Here it is reissued on Rawax alongside the Mood Mix, which is a deeper, dubber, warmer, more soulful sound that is just as good but for different settings, like a close-knit back room locked in for the long haul. … Read more

 Drumaturgy
 Review: Johannes Volk discovered Detroit techno when he was just 14, then soaked up the clubs between Kassel and Frankfurt before landing his first record on Jeff Mills's Axis label in 2006. A few years later, he started his own Exploration imprint, and now he's a vinyl-obsessed and conceptual producer dropping this third Running Back outing. It has five tracks in various states of consciousness: 'Shining Force' feels like exploring a strange planet, 'Switching from Bicycle to Spaceship' leaves gravity behind and 'Back Alley Dancers' smears half-remembered vocal fragments. 'Drumatury' ends with Chicago-style snare rolls to complete a playful, mind-bending outing. … Read more

 La Porte De L'Enfer
 Review: Earring presents a time capsule of work made in their 20s and 30s, here pieced together as an 'audio essay' responding to the ideas of Danish philosopher Soren Kierkegaard. Also known as the Father of Existentialism, the academic theorist posited that "anxiety is the vertigo of freedom", and across seven tracks it becomes increasingly easy to understand how that has been interpreted sonically. Jittering synth lines giving way to unapologetic four-to-the-floor grooves that feel unstable and vulnerable but also headstrong and determined. Like re-entering Earth's atmosphere in a homemade rocket that will survive, but with a good amount of damage. Spoken word messages with echoed ambient refrains. Uncompromising peak time techno. Haunting sound waves. A record that feels uneasy, but not oppressive.

 Review: Bleep-originating Sheffield is back on the map, as fledgling outing Short Span now stock over a dozen premium quality "tunes on discs" since Sa Pa's 2025 release 'The Fool'.  Review: You have to admire EXARDE's no-nonsense approach to the label's popular compilation series. Where other imprints do single vinyl EPs, they do untitled doublepacks, squeezing in as many high-grade cuts as possible. There's naturally plenty of highlights across the fifth volume in the series, from the moody new beat-meets-EBM flex of Acid Drift's 'Pulse Driver' and the vintage Belgian techno-inspired house throb of Booh's 'Red Curtain', to the Cabaret Voltaire-goes-to-Amsterdam flex of Gushi's 'Roto' and the sweat-soaked analogue muscularity of Luca's 'Space Haze Maze', with its chugging EBM grooves and wickedly intense TB-303 acid lines. SV3's 'Desconocido', a deeply psychedelic and moody nod to Nitzer Ebb with added acid trickery, is also sublime. … Read more

 GSG - "Higher Spiritual Planes" (5:06)
 Review: The mighty Delsin continue their deep dive into Dutch techno history with the second instalment of their Djax-Up-Beats 1990-2005 series. The NL Connection gathers 25 tracks exploring the homegrown talent that helped shape the label's early years, from Terrace's smooth opener ''Seventh City' and Trance's darker, more throbbing 'Inductio' to Planet Gong's fizzing percussion, thumping drums and celestial bleeps on 'Subzero Gravity'. The whole thing has been masterfully curated by Rush Hour co-founder Christiaan Macdonald and in moving from melodic machine music to raw acid, jackhammer techno and stranger underground excursions show just how much range this vital label had from the off. With fresh mastering and detailed liner notes, it's an essential homage to a great label. … Read more

 Thurlow Joyce - "Sundowner"
 Review: Astro runs Neptune Discs from Edinburgh with the idea that the experiments of 90s and early-2000s club music are unfinished business. 50-plus releases in, this 12-producer double LP proves it's a mission worth joining if you haven't already. Wigs and Aiden Francis bring warped, driving prog, COZi's 'Ride the Snake' is a lean tech-house roller with turn-of-the-millennium economy, and Jeku's 'Droop' oozes ozone. Thurlow Joyce closes with 'Sundowner' which is pure submerged dub-techno warmth. It's psychedelic and mind-expanding but also nostalgic without being overly stuck in what came before. A proper trip whether on headphones or cranked up in the club. … Read more
